## Remaining software gates implemented and unit-tested · October 11 UTC

Every gate in the new [PARITY table](PARITY.md) is **implemented + unit-tested, awaiting Ryan's real test**. None is device-verified.

1. **Test isolation (`6cae83f`).** The default suite's package-redirection test used to write and delete a fixture profile in the live `%LOCALAPPDATA%\EasyEject\Profiles`. Each test process now uses its own `%USERPROFILE%\.codex\EasyEjectFixtures\appdata-<id>`, inherited by child processes and removed on exit. A new test fails if `Profiles.Root` resolves inside the real LocalAppData. Production leaves the override null.
2. **Gates (`448574e`).**
   - A shared `AppQuitPolicy` drives tray close and Quit in both interfaces. Close hides to the tray, and Quit exits only when idle with no open editor.
   - A refused Quit is now forgotten. Before this, the classic app stayed armed, so a later window close exited.
   - New default-suite cases:
     - native-helper 4K Quick Share with exact reuse
     - a separate-process app session, Quit, then restart-create and restart-reuse on the production default root with the source absent
     - an unsaved draft survives a refused Quit
     - the no-media eject choice keeps Keep Connected as the default
     - a simulated travel run: sleep mid-copy, wake, reconnect, fresh retry, reuse, and history after the source is removed
   - Modern static theme/DPI contract: theme-resource brushes only, no forced theme, PerMonitorV2.
3. **DPI.**
   - The three Forms layout tests read the live monitor scale, so they failed when the display was temporarily at 225%. They now run at fixed 100/150/225% through `DpiForm.FixedScaleForTests`, whatever the monitor is at: the real-DPI layout runs first, then bounds and text are scaled by the remaining ratio.
   - The 225% run reproduced a real defect. When the device window cannot reach its preferred size, the wrapped action buttons took the whole height and left the storage list at 1 px. The action row is now capped to the remaining room (at least one button row) and scrolls.
   - An earlier attempt using a fixed 96-DPI thread context hung the .NET 10 runner on an exception during form load. That process was stopped and its run (`Claude-20261011-0417`) is discarded.

Final full run `C:\Users\rsmit\.codex\EasyEjectBuildTools\Claude-20261011-0439`, warnings as errors, BelowNormal priority:
- modern selected **246/0**

## Mac deletion-race parity check (07de127) · October 10 UTC

Mac `07de127` closes path-based races between final verification and deleting an original, a recovered Trash file or cleanup metadata. Windows already uses the safe design for all of these. Each item is opened without following reparse points, with no sharing on removal, so it cannot be renamed, replaced or written while held. The final path, volume serial and file type are checked, the bytes are re-verified, and the item is deleted through its own handle (`FileDispositionInfo`), never by a path or recursively. Parents stay held, cleanup skips reparse points, and `__MACOSX` is removed only when empty and only atomically.

`DeletionRaceParityTests.cs` (`--deletion-races`, **3/0**) mirrors Mac cases 1, 2 and 4/5 at the last audit step before removal. A swap to a directory or another file is refused by the OS. An ancestor swapped for a junction never reaches the outside folder. Mac cases 3 and 6–8 map to existing Windows Trash and cleanup tests named in the file. No production change was needed. Isolated copy `ClaudeRaceParity-20261010T093504Z`, identical to this commit: modern app build clean, selected **242/0**, full modern **1430/0**, and Framework full **1430/0**.

## Open Folder in import history for DJI O4 Pro batches · October 10 UTC

Mirroring Mac `57f5e07`, both interfaces' import history now shows **Open Folder** beside Quick Share for opted-in DJI O4 Pro batches. `CompletionFolder.Open(CompletedBatch…)` validates the exact saved batch and refuses a missing folder. It holds the destination root and batch folder, reserves the destination disk, revalidates the destination guard, and only then opens Explorer. A moved or replaced folder or a disconnected destination opens nothing. Opening grants no source, eject or sharing authority.

## Port DJI O4 Pro Gyroflow-first Quick Share from latest Mac · October 10 UTC

The Mac commits after the `384cb767` Windows baseline (`af70f83`, `57f5e07`, `dc2c3ba`, fetched read-only from `origin/main`) were not yet on Windows; they are now ported in the shared runtime and both interfaces. Each device profile has an explicit camera choice: Insta360 Luna by default, with the field absent so existing profiles keep their bytes, or DJI O4 Pro. DJI O4 Pro devices are Gyroflow-first. Their completion choice explains opening the import folder and batch stabilizing in Gyroflow, and does not offer Quick Share there. Import history still offers it afterwards.

Manual Quick Share then shares only the default `_stabilized.mp4` Gyroflow exports beside verified O4 Pro originals in the exact batch. A missing export refuses the whole action and names the originals still to export, both in the prompt pre-check and again in the engine. Originals must also match the O4 Pro name pattern and probe as encoder `DJI O4P`, and export durations must be within 0.5 s. Exports are hashed and held read-only with their originals through publication. Receipts bind the export hash under a separate DJI recipe. A changed re-export gets a new conflict-named copy and keeps the earlier one. The optional official DJI O4 D-Log M LUT is saved in Quick Share tools by its exact SHA-256 and required only when it is actually applied. Gyroflow's explicit `Gyroflow export LUT applied:` comment line keeps the existing colors. A LUT that does not match the device's camera is refused. Luna behavior, recipe and LUT file names are unchanged.

Nullable `AutomaticUpdateChecks` migrates absent legacy values to on and preserves explicit off across Save/reload/unrelated edits. The minimum-size scrollable settings view adds the checkbox and honest approved-channel/privacy/manual-installation explanation; Cancel writes nothing and other settings retain their choices. Saving does not fetch metadata synchronously. A normal monitored app admits the first configured check when idle, then uses a one-day cadence measured from each admitted attempt, including failure/cancellation. Busy imports/ejects/scans/disabled owners/owned modal dialogs defer due checks; idle admits once. Duplicate manual checks refuse and timer pulses do not overlap. Cancellation/disposal are synchronized; cancellation callback faults are observed without restoring scope. Failure frees the slot for another explicit attempt, while the automatic cadence prevents a one-second retry loop. After worker completion, foreground delivery checks host/activity/preference/request identity and is one-use; queued results cannot replace newer requests or survive intervening activity. Incoming offer expiry is checked again before coordinator acceptance. Future manual review actions still need fresh offer/native/file checks; accepted metadata is not installation authority.

`WindowsReleaseChecks` explicitly constructs code-approved discovery with the held HTTPS transport, exact manifest trust and a lazily shared native CurrentUser replay store under resolved `Profiles.Root`. The root is not resolved before an admitted configured request. There is deliberately **no production channel/publisher configured**, so real app automatic/manual availability remains honest and no request occurs. Read-only windows start no release timer. Configured background checks remain independent of import busy state: starting storage work cancels metadata authority rather than blocking the import. Completion updates only release status; it never replaces storage progress/issues or opens a dialog. A newer stable release may use the existing notification preference and delivery, which only opens the app; development/unknown local versions are not declared current. Closing a live host stops its timer and revokes pending checks. Full foreground/manual review and actual configured endpoint operation remain unproved.

### Persistent user-bound Windows channel replay restrictions · goal continuation

Commit `422a34ecf24bb9ed3518b374afa5fc2af08cdd3d` adds `WindowsChannelReplay.cs`, composes optional durable acceptance with discovery/process high water, and extends the private configuration writer with an exact-previous-file validation callback. Inspected Git/current progress/parity/README/distribution, strict signed channel and existing configuration/native publication contracts first. Application production publisher/channel/checks remain unconfigured. The source adapter must explicitly supply this store under resolved `Profiles.Root`; constructor-only/test process checks can still deliberately omit it. No default app data or real endpoint was used by these tests.

`windows-channel-replay.json` holds only an accepted restriction: format, approved-policy SHA-256, highest issue/body hash, numeric stable version and SHA-256 over every immutable release field. It contains no saved channel body/signature/archive, URL action, install permission or cached offer. Both endpoint locations, archive base and approved publisher subject/issuer pins enter a domain-separated length-prefixed policy hash and protection entropy. Native Framework [CurrentUser DPAPI](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.security.cryptography.protecteddata?view=netframework-4.8.1) protects/authenticates the floor; there is no plaintext or machine-scope fallback and no supplied password/key/account. Protection output is bounded and roundtrip identity-checked before publication. Strict UTF-8/no-BOM JSON/schema/base64 and native ordinary-file/identity/alias/protection/stream checks bound envelopes to 32 KiB, protected bytes to 16 KiB and plaintext to 1 KiB. Malformed/unavailable/wrong-policy/unprotectable state remains intact for review.

Fresh offer time and exact cryptographic/native publisher trust are required before storage. Historic metadata expiry does not discard the floor; new offers still must be fresh and valid. Every acceptance reacquires the shared native profile writer, reloads/authenticates current state and rejects earlier issues, same-issue body equivocation, lower versions and changed immutable versions. Equivalent floor checks preserve bytes without encryption/publication; later valid renewal can advance issue time. Another store/editor cannot overwrite a concurrently admitted newer release; contention reports refusal for manual retry. Root/directory leases survive protection/publication checks. The writer's new callback compares full prior hash and native stamp while its exact previous file is exclusively held, including mutations with restored timestamp/size. Initial absence cannot overwrite a late appearing file. The existing nonforced configuration rename/flush/owned temporary cleanup is retained; arbitrary external mutation during the necessary old-handle-close/rename boundary is not an OS compare-and-swap guarantee.

Cancellation/scope/expiry failures before publication preserve prior state and release the check/writer slot. After publication, cancellation or final-check failure retains the restrictive floor; failure reports that state was published, and a subsequent discovery return-scope guard refuses an offer if activity changed. A previously observed deletion or restored earlier valid record refuses within the same store. **DPAPI is not monotonic hardware storage:** a user can delete/restore whole state, and a new process cannot reconstruct a missing record. Normal restart replay protection assumes retained state. State/profile backup, Windows-user changes and code-approved channel/publisher policy changes need explicit recovery/migration review; there is no silent reset/migration. Actual cross-user/profile migration has not been exercised. This store cannot approve a release from its saved state.

Initial targeted result **21/9**: eight follow-on fixtures reused an issue timestamp for changed versions and correctly hit equivocation before their intended boundary; corrected their chronology and asserted publication-boundary reach. The ninth was a directory-shaped record's native-open error rather than a useful typed refusal; added explicit protected/reparse/directory preflight. Final `./Windows/build.ps1 -Test` passes **846/0**, including all **36** replay cases, with warnings as errors/process exit zero; `%USERPROFILE%\.codex\EasyEjectWindowsPreview\windows-replay-final-test.log`. Real CurrentUser protection roundtrip/tamper/wrong-policy refusal is observed; positive release/publisher trust and most protection failure seams are fake. All files/aliases/streams are owned disposable fixtures; no actual endpoint request, candidate execution or device action occurred.

### Held-file Windows native signature and approved publisher primitives · goal continuation

Commit `afa999f4e240012898a5d347a45ec064da46e0ac` implements `WindowsAuthenticode.cs` as the native trust layer for later read-only candidate validation. Re-read Mac `UpdateChannel.swift`/`UpdateSafetyTests.swift` and the Windows manual delivery contract. This layer is not connected to a configured release channel, a whole-package verifier or any install/launch operation. No Windows publisher has been selected or implicitly approved.

`WinVerifyTrust` receives the existing read-only candidate handle and canonical path, requests no UI, cached-only trust retrieval, revocation checking and explicit primary signature selection. The strong signature policy requests RSA/SHA-256/384/512 with at least 2048-bit RSA, or ECDSA/SHA-256/384/512 with at least 256-bit keys, including strong CRL/OCSP checks. Exact zero is required: positive nonzero LONG results are failures as well as negative trust errors. Every attempted verify closes its native state before returning, including verify/signer exceptions and incomplete proof; close failure cannot return approval. Provider signer/leaf/issuer certificates are read only from the successfully verified live state, copied into immutable SHA-256 evidence before closing, and an unsupported signer digest refuses. System DLL lookups are constrained to System32. There is no certificate-store import, trust bypass, network fallback, execution, extraction or automatic retry.

Publisher approval binds the exact SHA-256 of the leaf's DER subject and verified issuing certificate; signer evidence separately retains the leaf certificate hash for later manifest/executable agreement. A familiar name or another trusted signer is insufficient. Missing approval refuses before opening/creating candidate storage. The file review retains canonical/native ancestor leases and a regular single-link read-only file with no write/delete sharing, limits candidate size to 1 GiB, compares complete SHA-256 before/after trust, rechecks identity/stamp/link/offline/path evidence, and releases on refusal/cancellation/disposal. Its later `Validate` is an identity/stamp check; a whole-package action must still recheck its authenticated hashes. Cancellation is checked around the synchronous cached native trust call, not an interrupt inside Windows trust processing.

### Windows manual installation/update/rollback view and delivery contract · goal continuation

Commit `6fa5b8676292eadd85384415c7ea6b230356fd66` adds **Windows updates & installation** to the tray and Help. Read `UpdateChannel.swift` and `UpdateSafetyTests.swift`: Mac checks refuse active import/eject/modal/sheets, downloads/install remain user-selected, and approved Sparkle relaunch resumes once after idle. The Windows delivery direction remains manual update/rollback first. The new view makes that path visible while stating that this development build has **no approved Windows release channel or publisher** and cannot determine whether newer Windows releases exist. It does not claim “up to date,” use Mac assets, queue an install or resume/restart when idle.

The view reports development version and separately resolved application/settings folders, future approved Windows x64 ZIP/hash/publisher checks, complete-folder extraction, explicit Quit versus hide, settings backup, retained version folders, moved-copy startup review, format-aware rollback and app-folder-only removal. Close is the Enter/Escape default. Folder buttons are explicit read-only views: captured canonical/native directory leases are validated again at each click, missing/redirected locations disable their own buttons without creating storage, changed empty folders refuse, viewer failures retain instructions and do not retry, and disposed views release leases/refuse stale callbacks. ### Owned configuration publication, cleanup and per-folder writer lifetime · goal continuation

Commit `37b17480b37b86a579c2a4adbfbdd8d0b5ad4d66` addresses the configuration writer found in the directory audit. Read Mac `DroneImportManager.persist` (atomic JSON publication before updating live profiles), `DroneProfile.swift` and `ImportDefaults.swift`, plus the Windows profile/settings/history/tool setup callers before editing. Five disposable pre-change cases failed (**0/5**, `%USERPROFILE%\.codex\EasyEjectWindowsPreview\config-writer-before.log`): temporary overwrite before publication, released temporary rename/foreign cleanup, late final-name overwrite, hard-linked final replacement and release of the published handle before completion.

`ConfigurationWriter` now validates its JSON filename/value/type before storage creation and validates the nearest existing ancestor before creating missing private configuration children. It retains canonical/native directory proof, an owned exclusive temporary handle through serialization/flush/native publication/final flush, and a typed live per-folder `ProfileWriter`. General settings/defaults/history writes acquire their own writer; enrolled/offline profile and Quick Share setup transactions reuse their existing exact-root writer without reacquisition or releasing the caller's lease. Wrong-root/closed writers refuse before storage creation. Existing configuration is held exclusively from initial admission through serialization; protected/offline/system/reparse/hard-linked files refuse explicitly. An initially absent final name uses nonreplace publication, so a late foreign occupant is retained. Unpublished cleanup validates and deletes only its own held regular single-link temporary; newly protected/aliased partials remain with an exact-path warning. Late final-check/flush failures report that publication already occurred.

Native tests showed Windows error **32** when attempting replacement while retaining an exclusive old-file handle. The final implementation closes its own old validator only inside the ordinary native commit boundary while the per-folder writer remains held; the incoming temporary stays exclusive throughout. It uses standard `FileRenameInfo` replacement only for private configuration, with no POSIX/forced-sharing or path-based fallback. Media publication still uses nonreplace rename. External read/write locks refuse acquisition, competing Easy Eject writers refuse and require manual retry, and late protected final attributes are checked immediately before commit. Native rename failures now include the Windows error number. This is atomic name publication under the app's writer, **not an OS-wide compare-and-swap against arbitrary external processes during the required old-handle release**. [Microsoft FILE_RENAME_INFO](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/ns-winbase-file_rename_info) and [SetFileInformationByHandle](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-setfileinformationbyhandle) describe the native API; the actual local fixture results establish the tested behavior. Delete-pending owned writer handles can report zero links, so validation binds their captured native identity/empty length/canonical path and accepts that state only after the owner marked deletion pending. Occupied nonempty or aliased lock files remain intact.

Twenty-four new cases cover temporary/old/published handle exclusivity, owned failure cleanup, late conflict refusal, final/native identity, external locks, serializer failures with present/absent settings, root replacement, invalid/null/redirected/missing/occupied paths, initial and late protection, retained protected/aliased temporaries, existing/absent competing writers and manual retries, precise late-commit reporting and live/wrong/closed/borrowed/occupied/aliased writer lifetime. An intermediate full run passed **647/1**, `config-writer-verified-test.log`: the older read-only offline-save case expected a filesystem `UnauthorizedAccessException`. Updated that assertion to the writer's explicit `SafetyException`; its exact hash, read-only flag, forgotten-profile refusal and no-private-work assertions remain unchanged.

Final `./Windows/build.ps1 -Test` passes **649/0** with warnings as errors, `%USERPROFILE%\.codex\EasyEjectWindowsPreview\config-writer-final-test.log`; `--configuration-writer` passes **24/0**, `config-writer-final-targeted.log`. `git diff --check` passed and no tree outside Windows changed relative to the pinned Mac baseline. Builds and all fixtures remain outside OneDrive. No real media/device, startup, signing or publishing operation occurred.

### Automatic admission through continuous source proof and accurate preflight waits · goal continuation

Commit `12f7f5351246cd6092b64b9a03dfc89113627969` closes the remaining automatic-admission global epoch gap. Read Mac `DroneImportManager` per-volume connection invalidation, attempted/waiting reset on storage events and `start`/worker volume/root validation before editing. Windows automatic source tokens now include the complete source removal group's disk/volume topology and source bus/removable/hotplug properties as well as its original volume/disk fingerprints and connection stamp. `RequireCurrent` accepts only that same frozen token/root, returns a separate fresh observation and rejects ambiguous duplicate source IDs. A global epoch change alone no longer claims that an unchanged source reconnected; formatting, replacement, mount/permission/sibling/hidden-partition changes and source tracking loss still refuse.

New `AutomaticImportAdmission.Bind` requires the exact frozen profile's source/destination folders, checks its enrolled source and saved destination, and builds fresh source/destination protection. The internal selection callback crosses a newer global epoch only after a live scoped proof of the original source group agrees with the unchanged token. Cached inventory without a live provider cannot bridge that epoch. Constructor races, source reconnects and unavailable evidence refuse. The public/manual volume-guard constructor retains its strict selection epoch; this is an automatic source-proof path, not a weakening of user review. Guards still reserve all source/destination/sibling physical disks and retain conservative global fallback when scoped evidence is initially unavailable.

Preflight waiting now records the epoch actually observed during binding (including before native path resolution and fresh scans), rather than the request's older admission epoch. An already-observed unrelated event cannot immediately loop the same preflight failure. A later storage event permits one preflight retry, matching the Mac waiting behavior; started/runtime/manual/cancelled attempts remain consumed on that connection. An old request cannot rewrite a newer source connection's attempt. Default automatic import stays off; no profile, startup or device settings were enabled.

Successful secondary actions do not clear primary issue state. Completed import/share/verification presentation waits for the separate manual action's terminal state, and quitting/settings/automatic starts remain blocked while either action runs. Confirmed removal invalidates only related process completion sources. No automatic eject or retry was added.

### Protected connection proof for import and saved-batch guards · goal continuation

Commit `a0404da450ac715f9020140e393d1c6d3424ced3` starts the exact Mac `DroneImportManager.blocksEject`/`reserveManualEject` behavior. Protected volume serial/extents/mount/system changes, disk identity/devnode/bus/removable/hotplug changes, added hidden volumes/LUNs, reconnects with unchanged identities and tracking loss refuse. Fresh complete scans and live stamps must agree. Unrelated epochs are acknowledged only after every protected group remains unchanged; event races/incomplete scans refuse. If any initial snapshot/live provider evidence is unavailable, the original global-epoch refusal remains, with whole-group topology checks still required. A lost scoped proof never downgrades to fallback during an operation.

`VolumeImportGuard` and `WindowsDestinationGuard` use the proof; public import callers still resolve real native volume IDs. Internal test seams provide fake path resolution only to disposable tests. Protected disk arrays are defensive copies. Native discovery now records instance proof for internal destination disks as well as external/removable groups. The saved-batch guard protects only the destination and its sibling LUNs, so an absent/ejected source never becomes export or eject authority. Shared operation reservations remain unchanged and protect source, destination and related LUNs throughout copying.

Twenty-four new cases in `tests/StorageConnectionTests.cs` cover unrelated events/disappearance, a fake unrelated eject during a 2.2 MB disposable transfer with source/destination reservations still busy, protected disk/USB/controller/sibling reconnects, missing/partial/lost tracking and no-live-provider fallback, live stamp change without a global epoch, topology/identity/path changes, incomplete/racing scans, stale initial stamps/group coverage, destination-only and removable-destination sibling proof, and interruption/partial cleanup/reservation release. No real eject or media operation occurred.

Implementation: `128ac4d5491889e1c6e791e214707bb22baba9ad`. Inspected exact Mac `DroneImportManager.menuTitle` and `AppLifecycle.updateStatus`/busy menu heading. All import/automatic import/resume/history verification/cleanup/eject/Quick Share callbacks capture an immutable operation token. Cancellation keeps a stopping state; late callbacks from a cancelled, completed or replaced operation cannot overwrite window text or tray results. Import transitions to a fresh history/finalization phase before awaiting persistence, invalidating old copying callbacks. Sharing seals its token before presenting success/failure/cancellation. Final result dialogs cannot receive earlier worker progress, and cancellation capability is disabled when the activity ends. No tray status row starts an operation or bypasses the existing review/safety flows.

Help/About/debug menus and callbacks refuse while work or scans are active.

The debug viewer now offers explicit Refresh, Copy to Clipboard and Clear log. Opening it performs neither copying nor clearing. Copy uses the displayed immutable text rather than silently reading newer events; clipboard failures retain the text. Clear opens only the fixed private `debug.log`, holds it exclusively and compares its exact content hash and native identity/size/modified stamp with the displayed snapshot. Changed/new/replaced logs refuse until an explicit Refresh and review. It marks only the held log for deletion; no directory sweep, preference/history removal or media action occurs. Existing append/read paths now also reject hard links and offline/system files. Read-only logs remain readable, but append/clear preserve their protection and refuse. Oversized, redirected, directory-shaped or concurrently held logs are kept. Empty/missing states and absent action capabilities disable mutation/copy appropriately; read-only smoke/render callers never access the real clipboard or clear private state.

### Full manual Quick Share history/completion integration · goal continuation

Implementation commit: `60b92c7d018d1374f5598e101e92afcc94b46376`.

**Quick Share…** is now an app action on a selected history batch when its current device profile opts in, and on a completed import with an exact successfully saved history batch. Verification history remains available when sharing is off; later enabling reveals earlier retained batches, while disabling/forgetting hides sharing. Import/history persistence now captures the exact batch for completion rather than confusing the independent process eject ID with the receipt/history ID. Completion stores an immutable batch and validates its source-profile, destination identity/root and folder. Index-only/no-media or failed-history completions cannot acquire sharing eligibility.

Manual admission re-reads the last-20 history and current valid enabled profile, compares the complete selected batch snapshot and loads the chosen tool setup. A fresh Cancel-default prompt freezes this action's 1080p/4K and LUT choice. After OK, the service rechecks history/opt-in, acquires the exact configuration/tool leases, then checks history/opt-in again after potentially long executable hashing. It builds a fresh destination guard and invokes the existing full transaction with destination reservations. Original/source presence is unnecessary; no earlier folder scanning or restored eject authority is introduced. Changed/expired history, disabled/forgotten/corrupt profiles, changed/missing executables or destination refuse. Preferences edited during a prompt do not silently replace its explicit choices. A service busy gate independently rejects duplicate work.

App integration keeps the busy state until the action finishes, provides cancellation and suppresses queued progress after its operation ends. Clean success clears prior issue state, reports new/reused copies or no supported Luna masters, and respects notification preferences. Export failure retains exact retry history and reports precise publication/private-work counts. Normal cancellation keeps existing issue state; unmatched output/receipt publication or retained private-work warnings preserve attention. Import/save/refresh/notification clicks do not invoke export. Each retry is another manual prompt; full-quality imported originals remain unchanged.

Success invoked from completion returns only to the same still-present process entry and recomputes current eject/cleanup eligibility before a fresh Keep Connected/default choice, including the sharing outcome. Source expiry during export prevents that return. Failure or prompt/work cancellation never enters the eject branch. Saved-history sharing remains available after source eject/restart and does not recreate process authority. Source expiry disables only the current dialog's eject button; any saved-copy sharing still passes current history/profile/tool/destination admission. Read-only history/completion windows exclude sharing actions.

### Explicit tool selection, official LUT copy and guarded Quick Share setup · goal continuation

Implementation commit: `cbaa8fd2d67c2742a25234f57a79501467b922b4`.

Settings now opens a separate **Quick Share tools…** window with explicit local .exe/.cube pickers, clear-selection actions and a Cancel-default footer. Saving this window separately records selected executable paths, sizes and SHA-256 without running any executable or changing another application setting. Setup can be partial, but export admission requires both executables. Save validation runs in an owned background task, disables resubmission/choice changes and waits for cancellation before closing. A late Cancel after configuration commit reports success accurately. Read-only settings exclude the action. Merely opening either settings window, changing a picker or cancelling does not persist choices or start an export.

The versioned setup JSON is bounded, canonical and separate from profiles/app settings. Missing setup remains unconfigured without PATH discovery. Configuration-only reload does not open disconnected executables. Stale editors, changed/replaced setup files and malformed/oversized records refuse. Save uses the existing private configuration writer gate. Manual acquisition holds the exact chosen configuration, pinned executable/LUT parents and read-only file handles through export; size/hash mismatch or unavailable tools requires reselection. Normal-color export does not depend on an unused optional LUT. Saving unrelated settings preserves setup, and clear-selection actions retain supplied executables and original media.

Choosing and saving the official manufacturer LUT explicitly validates the baseline SHA-256 and publishes a flushed, exclusive local app-data copy with its content hash in the name. The user's downloaded original stays intact; exports can use the local copy after the download is moved. An existing local copy must validate; unexpected content is not overwritten. Clearing LUT preferences deletes neither asset. Failed/cancelled setup can retain an already published official local copy, while previous configuration remains unless its write committed. No LUT or tool is bundled/downloaded/installed automatically.

### Guarded Quick Share export, exclusive receipts, verified reuse and manual retry · goal continuation

Implementation commit: `231cc4b8db17c179a7b3ff9eecfb3a843ae9714d`.

Added the full export transaction behind the forthcoming manual UI. Admission deep-copies and validates the exact completed history/receipt/destination, reserves the guard's destination disks, pins every saved parent path and holds the completed receipt and all recorded full-quality originals read-only through the transaction. The complete saved batch is SHA-256 checked before any tool starts, including noncandidate files. Source-device presence is unnecessary and no eject authority is restored. Only recorded Luna masters can convert; other destination/date files are never scanned. No candidates creates no sharing/audit/tool/LUT work. Candidates inside Sharing Copies refuse. Explicitly supplied executable paths remain pinned/held across the operation, and I-Log requires the official LUT bytes before work. The current clip is rehashed and every original's held stamp checked before publication/reuse; the complete batch is rehashed before final success. This avoids rehashing every large original for each clip while preserving full-batch admission/final proof and held-file protection.

For each candidate the transaction probes input, freezes the Windows recipe, checks estimated export space plus 1 GiB, encodes into a new exclusive private folder, probes the output and requests complete video/audio decode. Output validation now also requires an MP4 container and exactly the intended video/audio streams. A read-only output handle prevents changes during probe/decode; after it closes, an exclusive READ/WRITE/DELETE handle must match the same file identity/stamp/size/hash before flush/publication. Byte-identical replacement with restored time refuses. Fixed private LUT naming avoids filter-path interpolation. Originals, receipt, tools, directories and disk reservations remain held; no source/original protection is changed.

Output and recipe/source/LUT/output-hash receipts publish through non-replacing native handle renames and explicit flushes. Existing/unverifiable/changed exports and occupied receipt names remain intact; up to 100 stable SHA-prefix conflict names are considered. Verified matching receipts require current recipe/preset/color/LUT/source, dimensions/rate/duration, size and output SHA-256; reused output/receipt handles stay held through audit/result acceptance. New output/receipt handles likewise remain held through completion audit. A separately flushed per-attempt audit persists under Sharing Copies/.easy-share-audits. Publication of the two files is deliberately tracked separately: interruption after output rename retains that verified file without falsely claiming receipt completion. Failures/cancellation report actual created/reused and published-file/receipt counts, folder/audit and any retained private work. Retry is manual, revalidates the saved batch and reuses only confirmed matching copies. Receipt races, late audit failures and later-clip cancellation preserve earlier publications.

Cleanup touches only known regular eligible files in the current exclusively created private folder, then verifies its identity and requests empty-directory disposition. Unknown items, protected/linked files and nonempty/unavailable directories are retained with a warning; no recursive deletion or attribute/permission bypass occurs. A newly created private directory that cannot be guarded is retained and identified in its error. Sharing copies and durable audits remain after cancellation/failure. The transaction has no device-eject API or automatic caller; per-device/manual admission UI remains pending.

Twenty-eight new cases cover probe/complete-decode ordering, bound receipt/audit publication, 1080p/4K/reuse, native runner orchestration/file-sharing with this project's synthetic tool helper, held reuse/new publications, last-20 history after source absence/restart and no date rescans, whole-batch/changed destination/receipt/manifest refusal, original/receipt/tool mutation denial, reservation overlap/release, unsupported/no-candidate clips, changed/foreign receipts/outputs and 100 conflicts, probe/decode/HDR failure, mid-encode/final-stage/later-clip cancellation, destination loss, free-space/official-LUT requirements, byte-identical temporary replacement, late output/receipt races, partial publication counts/manual retry, late audit failure, sharing junctions, unknown private data and protected temporary retention. Existing recipe cases additionally refuse wrong/missing container and extra output streams. All data is synthetic or isolated disposable fixtures. Fixture teardown restores only its own deliberately read-only temporary after asserting production refusal; no real media/protection/device was changed.

**No actual video was encoded/decoded:** fake tools and the compiled synthetic protocol helper prove transaction/runner ordering and filesystem behavior, not FFmpeg/media acceptance. Real synthetic FFmpeg encoding/full decoding, positive official-LUT proof, confirmed camera mode and disposable-hardware/foreground/trusted-signing proof remain required. Quick Share still has no app action: explicit tool/LUT selection, independent off-default per-device offer/preferred choice migration, Cancel-default manual 1080p/4K/LUT prompt, saved-history/completion wiring, busy/cancellation/issue behavior and return to a fresh manual eject choice are next. ### Contained native Quick Share tool execution · goal continuation

Implementation commit: `dd57b99e787e8d84968c2119ca063a1fe66a920a`.

Ported the baseline process contract with a Windows-native runner for explicitly selected local executables. The executable and working-folder ancestors remain pinned and the executable is held read-only throughout execution. Arguments are quoted using Windows argv rules and passed to an explicit `CreateProcessW` module; there is no shell, PATH search, batch-script execution, elevation or security-policy change. The tool starts suspended, enters the app's own unnamed job with kill-on-close and no breakaway, then resumes. A handle whitelist passes only null standard input and the private stdout/stderr writers; unrelated inheritable handles are excluded. If job setup/assignment fails, the suspended process is stopped without permitting tool code to run; no uncontained fallback is offered.

Private exclusive file logs replace pipes. Separate reader/writer file objects preserve the child's output offset, writer sharing prevents outside mutation, and held-handle delete-on-close limits cleanup to this invocation's logs. Output is checked while waiting and after exit against the baseline 4,000,000-byte limit; captured reads allocate only from a checked length snapshot. This is a polled output limit, not a hard filesystem quota. Incremental UTF-8 progress works across reads/CRLF and retains the complete raw stdout result. Nonzero status reports only the last bounded stderr detail. Cancellation, guard loss, timeout, excessive output or progress failure stop the owned job and verify zero active workers plus parent exit before releasing resources. Successful parent exit also stops remaining worker descendants before accepting output. Final progress cancellation refuses success; no automatic retry occurs. The job contains worker lifecycle, not arbitrary executable permissions.

Validation: `./Windows/build.ps1 -Test` **369 passed; zero failed**, `/warnaserror+`; `git diff --check` passes. Twenty new cases launch only this project's copied helper executable in isolated disposable fixture folders and cover argv round-trips (spaces/Unicode/quotes/backslashes/metacharacters), explicit executable selection, exact working folder/null input, incremental progress/raw output, split UTF-8/CRLF, bounded stderr/exit codes, stdout/stderr floods, prelaunch/final/midrun cancellation, preflight/ongoing guard loss, timeout, callback failure, parent/descendant exit, successful-parent lingering-worker cleanup, unrelated handle exclusion, missing/invalid/locked/redirected executables/work directories and invalid argv/deadlines/script paths. Every exercised process log is gone afterward. Canonical fixture helper copies avoid the packaged host's redirected build-folder alias without weakening production path checks. Native job/handle/process tests pass on this laptop; no actual FFmpeg/FFprobe, camera/user clip or device adapter was used.

**Quick Share remains unavailable in the app.** The export transaction still must bind exact completed history/receipt/destination, reserve storage, hold/recheck originals, execute probe/encode/full decode, flush and exclusively publish output/receipts, verify reuse/conflict names and handle cancellation/partial publication/retry. Tool/LUT selection, per-device opt-in and manual preset/color prompt, history/completion UI and return-to-fresh-eject integration remain pending. Real synthetic encoder/full-decode and positive official-LUT/model/mode proof are required; no tool or LUT was installed/downloaded. No UI change in this milestone requires new layout renders. Full-port goal remains active; overnight heartbeat remains paused. No actual media import/removal/eject, startup installation, security/power change, Mac/website edit or publication occurred.

Primary Windows references checked: [CreateProcessW](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processthreadsapi/nf-processthreadsapi-createprocessw), [STARTUPINFOEX](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/ns-winbase-startupinfoexw), [handle-list attributes](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processthreadsapi/nf-processthreadsapi-updateprocthreadattribute), [job objects](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/procthread/job-objects), [nested jobs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/procthread/nested-jobs), [job accounting](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winnt/ns-winnt-jobobject_basic_accounting_information) and [argv parsing](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cpp/c-language/parsing-c-command-line-arguments?view=msvc-170). ### Luna Quick Share recipe, probe and official LUT rules · goal continuation

Implementation commit: `bafb9d85ce7035a8cf749584a357b4ec3c802be2`.

Actual encoder availability/runtime acceptance is still required.

The planned complete-decode command includes all selected video/audio with `-xerror` and duration-based deadlines; no decode was actually run here. LUT reading pins the selected local file/path, checks the exact baseline official SHA-256 and size, supports cancellation and returns held verified bytes for a future fixed private `luna.cube`. It does not bundle, install or fetch a LUT. Wrong/empty/oversized/redirected/locked LUTs refuse without changing them; no official LUT has been supplied for positive acceptance.

### Explicit per-user launch-at-login control and hidden tray startup · goal continuation

Implementation commit: `6c5bbe14f2100114038978d21b908f8a4b892f5b`.

Ported the Mac explicit launch-at-login choice to the unpackaged Windows app using its own `EasyEject.Windows` value in the current user's Run key. Reading Settings never creates or repairs registration. The checkbox starts off; only Save after an explicit change can register/remove a recognized app command. Quoted local executable paths and the sole `--startup` argument are validated; foreign/malformed entries are retained and cannot be overwritten or removed from this editor. A moved copy is reported and requires **Use this copy at login** before Save. Expected-value checks refuse changes detected while the editor or executable validation is in progress; these checks are optimistic, not an atomic registry compare-and-swap. Enable pins the current executable and its ancestors through the write/readback. Failures release handles, report a possibly applied change when readback is unavailable and never retry automatically. Other application preferences remain saved/applied if login registration fails.

Registration status is distinguished from Windows' actual enablement: Startup apps or policy may still disable/delay launch, and Easy Eject never overrides those controls. An explicit **Windows Startup apps…** button opens the documented settings URI. The recognized login invocation creates the tray/native notification handle without displaying the device window, schedules monitoring once and permits a later manual Show. Duplicate startup invocation quietly exits. Automatic import remains an independent off-default profile choice; enabled profiles may import when monitoring starts. Saving login registration itself starts no app/import/export/eject. No registration was installed during this development work.

### Device Trash ownership and original-path catalog · recovery implementation in progress

Implementation commit: `04bd63557a9d2f1ee1af3caff2d221d29ade11aa`.

After reading the Mac Trash recovery/selection/removal fixtures, added a read-only Windows-native source catalog for the next recovery integration. It selects only the current Windows user's SID under this device's `$RECYCLE.BIN`; `.Trashes/<UID>` is traversed only with an explicit numeric Mac-owner selection, never inferred from a Windows account. It never traverses other owners. Recycle Bin metadata maps randomized payloads back to original names and folder ancestry, preserving lens/sidecar and structured-package selection. Selected records retain captured metadata SHA-256/stamps and payload file identities. Unknown/unassociated payloads and device indexes never qualify for clearing; Windows folder records remain while their directories or retained contents remain. Windows and Mac names have separate recovery namespaces.

The bounded version-1/version-2 parser is based on [Joachim Metz's primary format research](https://github.com/libyal/dtformats/blob/main/documentation/Windows%20Recycle.Bin%20file%20formats.asciidoc), not a promised Microsoft storage-format contract. [WindowsIdentity.User](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.security.principal.windowsidentity.user?view=netframework-4.8.1) supplies the runtime owner. Future/invalid versions, malformed Unicode/length/time/size, unsupported original paths, duplicate case/Unicode-equivalent names, missing/orphan pairs, redirected/offline payloads and incorrect location types fail with a reviewable error. Throwing enumeration distinguishes absence from access failures. No Shell restore/empty operation or data removal is called.

### Guarded optional macOS metadata cleanup · goal continuation

Implementation commit: `334bf71a43dc7df158854c994266c4fcc62776ef`.

Read `MetadataCleaner.swift`, its cleanup preservation fixture, `DriveManager.eject`/bulk flow, `AppLifecycle.swift` and the cleanup acknowledgment in `SettingsView.swift`. Windows now ports optional removal of regular `._*`, `.DS_Store` and `.apdisk` files, plus empty `__MACOSX` directories. It traverses other directories without deleting arbitrary contents and preserves Mac/Windows Trash, Spotlight/events/temporary/system folders, all reparse points, system/offline items and hard-linked metadata aliases. Photos, recordings and other filenames remain intact. File disposition uses a held exclusive DELETE/attribute handle; ancestors stay pinned, canonical paths/types/volume serials are checked, and source identity is revalidated before deletion. Empty-folder removal compares the captured file identity and relies on Windows' atomic nonempty-directory refusal if new contents arrive. No read-only flag is cleared, permission changed, or recursive folder deletion used.

**Clean & eject…** explicitly reviews cleanup of the selected volume and all storage affected by removal. **Clean macOS metadata from cards before ejecting** starts off in new/legacy settings; enabling it requires a separate No-default acknowledgment, and saving never starts cleanup. Future card, bulk/shortcut and post-import eject confirmations include cleanup roots. Other external storage keeps plain eject unless Clean & eject is explicitly chosen; its warning starts on. Windows retains grouped removal confirmation even when the extra other-storage warning is disabled. Single-device cleanup covers the selected volume; bulk covers all confirmed card-volume roots, preserving the Mac scope distinction.

All group disks are reserved before cleanup. Bulk preflights every selected root before any deletion/request, keeps continuously verified peers across its own removals, and stops on cleanup failure while retaining earlier verified removal names/counts. Cancellation, changed roots/connections/topology, inaccessible or protected metadata stop the current removal request. Completed cleanup stays applied and its count is reported; no implicit retry follows. Post-import authority is consumed once before cleanup, so a failure cannot reuse that old action. The normal eject service now rescans after preparation, and cancellation is checked again at the API boundary. Debug/issue state carries the cleanup result/failure locally.

### Card groups, connection-aware bulk removal and optional shortcut · goal continuation

Implementation commit: `2571e98b508168f4d3fd20b8dc25010f9965e1ed`.

Read the baseline Mac classifier in `EjectorApp.swift`, `BulkEjectPolicy.swift`/its nine cases, `GlobalHotkeyManager.swift`, and grouped-unmount tests before implementation. Windows now separates camera candidates, emulator candidates, other external volumes, internal storage and protected Windows volumes. All eleven camera root names count case-insensitively from regular directories. Emulator classification needs two distinct supported root folders plus SD hardware evidence and takes precedence over camera evidence. Links/files do not supply evidence; fixed/system storage is never promoted. CFexpress/XQD remain explicit hardware candidates; generic NVMe plus DCIM does not prove CFexpress. Model/physical disk and mount information are shown, and card counts deduplicate removal devices across partitions/LUNs.

**Eject cards…** in the window/tray freezes all card groups in a scrollable confirmation; **Keep connected** is the Enter/Escape default. Mixed/unclassified siblings, hidden LUNs without classified volumes, protected/unknown/spanning mappings and unavailable connection proof refuse before removal. All confirmed disks are reserved together before any API. Requests run sequentially, revalidate all remaining groups, and verify each removed group's disks/volumes absent before continuing. A veto, changed peer or unverified disappearance stops later requests. The result preserves earlier verified-safe names; notifications lead with stopped/complete status, and failures persist in View issue. Newly connected cards are never added to an existing confirmation.

Read-only `CM_Register_Notification` all-device-instance events track target/disk/recorded-ancestor versions. Global epochs still guard ordinary import/eject/completion; bulk alone tolerates its own earlier device removal with continuously registered, unchanged proof for each remaining confirmed group immediately before native removal. Identical reconnects/restarts and shared-ancestor changes invalidate proof. Malformed events/registration gaps fault it until fresh registration. Callbacks only update counters; failed unregistration retains callback lifetime and suppresses actions.

The optional **Ctrl + Alt + Shift + D/E/F/G/K/M/R/T/X** shortcut starts off, including legacy settings, defaults to E, and opens the same fresh confirmation. Windows-key combinations are OS-reserved, hence different modifiers from Mac. Only the selected key is registered with `MOD_NOREPEAT`, releasing the previous registration first. Collisions/unsupported choices/release failures are explained without fallback keys. Busy/modal/scanning work and queued stale messages suppress activation. Saving settings installs no startup behavior.

### Connection-bound completion, notifications and issues · goal continuation

Implementation commit: `9191d525463248a761b3b82f216c07842488e62d`.

Profiles/shared defaults now expose **Ask to eject after import**, off initially and in older custom profiles. The choice freezes with media options for each operation. A completed scan can offer **Keep Connected** (Enter/Escape default), **Eject Now / Eject Device**, and **Open Import Folder**. No matching media explicitly warns that photos/skipped previews may remain. The grouped explanation names every affected partition/LUN. Import reservations are released before the choice. Completion authority captures the original source root, volume/disk identities, connection epoch and removal topology before copying; it stays in memory and is never restored from history/notifications. A new import invalidates its source action, device events invalidate all stale choices, and the removal request consumes its token once after reservation/revalidation. Busy disks retain the choice; vetoes do not retry.

**Completed imports…** in the window/tray preserves background choices when Windows notifications are suppressed. Notifications start on and debug logging off. Native NotifyIcon balloons reveal the app for review; clicking never requests removal or supplies device authority. When notifications are disabled, an opted-in post-import choice still uses the native dialog. Settings save only app preferences. Debug logging is local, explicitly enabled, flushed and capped at 1 MiB without deleting earlier diagnostics. **View issue…** persists the explanation and acknowledgment; Dismiss never retries, and even an identical later failure restores attention. Close hides the device window during a copy; quitting remains blocked while operations/dialogs are active. No startup registration is installed.

### Persistent exact batch history · goal continuation

Implementation commit: `01933cebfb27db2276a6bedad81105ba93c3825d`. Prior layout/reuse/audit milestone: `5f03d41`.

Completed media imports now save the newest 20 batches to `completed-imports.json` in the app's configuration folder. Each record includes the device/profile, completion date, exact verified saved paths/bytes/SHA-256, exact immutable receipt and its hash, destination root, volume GUID/filesystem serial and disk PnP/serial identity. Retention trims only history entries; it never deletes copies or receipts. Metadata-only/failed/cancelled imports do not enter history. A history persistence failure is reported separately while the verified import and its receipt remain available.

**Import history…** lists these batches even when the source is absent. Its explicit **Verify saved originals…** action obtains a fresh destination connection guard and shared disk reservations, pins paths, verifies receipt membership and hashes only the exact recorded files. It never scans the shared date folder for candidates or restores eject authority. Changed destination, receipt, saved original, fabricated membership or path redirection fails safely. Disk numbers/devnodes are not persisted as destination identity; new connections receive new guards. Quick Share itself is still pending.

### Date layouts, verified reuse and durable audit · goal continuation

Imports now share a `YYYY-MM-DD` destination folder. Clean layout is a third frozen profile/default choice, enabled for new/default profiles; earlier custom profiles retain their original tree behavior. Ordinary media groups flatten with filenames unchanged. Colliding filenames or recording associations move the whole group into a stable `Additional media/<fingerprint>` folder. New copies preserve camera modification dates and require caller-available space greater than file size plus 1 GiB. Every import has an exclusive `.easy-eject-receipts/<id>` record containing a flushed `import-audit.log` and its own `verified-import.json`; previous receipts are never overwritten. Failed/cancelled runs retain their audit and published copies, keep originals, and write no completion receipt. These flush checks do not establish physical power-loss durability.

Ryan reported execution ready after changing Smart App Control himself; readback `VerifiedAndReputablePolicyState=0`. The hidden Windows Forms smoke check creates the window, registers disk notifications, reads complete native inventory, and confirms system-volume ejection is blocked. No real card/media import or device removal was performed. Visual UI acceptance and physical hardware compatibility remain unverified.

Executed successfully:

```powershell
./Windows/build.ps1 -Test
& "$env:LOCALAPPDATA/EasyEject/PrototypeBuild/EasyEject.Tests.exe" --inventory
& "$env:LOCALAPPDATA/EasyEject/PrototypeBuild/EasyEject.Tests.exe" --ui-smoke
```

Initial runtime failures exposed two real defects: alternate-stream syntax reached Framework path parsing before the safety rejection, and `FILE_RENAME_INFO` lacked the terminating UTF-16 NUL required by the Win32 path-normalization wrapper. Both are fixed. Fixture files now use the ordinary `%USERPROFILE%\.codex\EasyEjectFixtures` directory outside OneDrive, avoiding the packaged host's app-data virtualization. Generic import-path checks remain strict. Production profile storage resolves only its own redirected configuration folder within local app-data and is covered by a passing save/load test. Tests now assert the intended failure stage for disconnection, tampering and junction rejection, instead of accepting an unrelated early path error. Large binary files, empty sidecars, byte totals, receipt hashes, profile replacement and post-format invalidation pass.

Ryan asked for independent overnight continuation. The app heartbeat `easy-eject-overnight-windows-port` is active every 30 minutes, bounded in its instructions to October 6 at 08:00 America/New_York. It may continue safe implementation/testing in this Windows checkout and update this report. No push, real-device operation, startup installation, or additional power/security change is authorized overnight. The Windows app and laptop must remain running/awake for local continuation.

## Delivered source

- Native Windows Forms tray/device UI; manual import/eject confirmations default to No. Close hides to tray; active operations prevent app exit; session mutex prevents duplicates.
- Read-only SetupAPI disk interfaces, volume GUID enumeration, volume-to-disk extents, storage descriptors, hotplug properties, PnP ancestry, and device-change generation.
- Conservative camera/card candidates. A generic SSD/NVMe device with camera folders is not certified as CFexpress. Internal drives remain protected.
- Manual `CM_Request_Device_EjectW` adapter with immediate devnode/instance revalidation and veto/error reporting. Plans include sibling partitions/LUNs and reject incomplete, spanning, changed or protected topology. No force fallback or real eject test.
- Shared source/destination disk reservations; verified folder copying with preserved relative paths, source stamps, SHA-256 rereads, exclusive handle-based publication, flushed writes, completion receipts, cancellation, and reparse/path-escape rejection. No source original deletion, cleanup, or auto-actions.
- Optional identity-bound import folder profiles. Windows first slice backs up the selected folder's regular files; Mac filters, clean layout, deduplication and other parity remain later work.
- 37 compiled test cases covering safety policy and disposable file fixtures, including same physical device, stale actions, changed topology, vetoes, path boundaries, conflicts, cancellation, source/destination tampering and junctions. **Zero cases executed on this laptop.**

## Runtime blocker: Windows Application Control

A tiny disposable program under the external build directory compiled successfully with the installed compiler. Its ordinary executable launch was rejected with:

> An Application Control policy has blocked this file.

Read-only `Microsoft-Windows-CodeIntegrity/Operational` logs contained event **3077** for the compiled `CompilerProbe.exe`, event **3033** describing the signing-level failure, and event **3118** labeled Smart App Control block details. Policy ID in the event: `{0283ac0f-fff1-49ae-ada1-8a933130cad6}`. Registry `HKLM\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Control\CI\Policy\VerifiedAndReputablePolicyState` is **1** (enabled). Windows reports Windows 11 Home build **26300**.

`CiTool.exe -lp -json` returned `OperationResult=-2147024891` (access denied); full policy provenance/possible additional organization policy could not be enumerated at the current privilege level. No elevation was requested. No allowlist, App Control, signing credentials, trust roots, or OS permissions were changed. No alternate assembly loader/interpreter was used to execute the blocked code. App and test launch are deferred rather than treated as successful.

Microsoft's [supported signing guidance](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/smart-app-control/code-signing-for-smart-app-control) calls for trusted-provider code signing, with Artifact Signing as a supported route. An authorized development-signing workflow or a separately approved test environment is required to run these binaries. No available trusted signing workflow was established here; no production credentials were inspected or used.

## Overnight readiness and OLED power settings

Ryan's latest clarification is simply to avoid a static image damaging the OLED, with actual panel power-off preferred when available. The one-minute AC display-off setting is retained. No further display/lock investigation is needed for this source milestone; the following records the checks already performed.

This Windows chat continued shell source/build work locally after the one-minute display timeout was configured; it does not depend on the Mac checkout being open. This proves current Windows-side activity, not guaranteed unattended scheduling after the chat stops or survival of power/network/app interruption. Foreground UI automation was deferred.

Windows' signed `System.Windows.Forms.SystemInformation.PowerStatus` reports **Online** (AC power). Active plan is **Turbo**, GUID `6fecc5ae-f350-48a5-b669-b472cb895ccf`.

| Setting | Original AC | Applied AC | Battery/DC |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Actual display-off timeout (`VIDEOIDLE`) | 300 seconds | **60 seconds** (`0x3c`) | 180 seconds, unchanged |
| Idle sleep timeout (`STANDBYIDLE`) | 300 seconds | **0 / never** | 180 seconds, unchanged |
| Hibernate timeout (`HIBERNATEIDLE`) | 0 / never | unchanged | unchanged |
| Lid close (`LIDACTION`) | 1 / sleep | unchanged | 1 / sleep, unchanged |
| Sign-in on wake (`CONSOLELOCK`) | 1 / yes | unchanged | 1 / yes, unchanged |

Exact mutations, read back successfully:

```powershell
powercfg /setacvalueindex SCHEME_CURRENT SUB_VIDEO VIDEOIDLE 60
powercfg /setacvalueindex SCHEME_CURRENT SUB_SLEEP STANDBYIDLE 0
powercfg /setactive SCHEME_CURRENT
powercfg /query SCHEME_CURRENT SUB_VIDEO VIDEOIDLE
powercfg /query SCHEME_CURRENT SUB_SLEEP STANDBYIDLE
powercfg /qh SCHEME_CURRENT SUB_BUTTONS LIDACTION
powercfg /qh SCHEME_CURRENT SUB_NONE CONSOLELOCK
```

Original settings were saved outside the repo at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\EasyEject\PrototypeBuild\power-before-2026-10-06.json`. To restore just the changed AC values on the same plan:

```powershell
powercfg /setacvalueindex 6fecc5ae-f350-48a5-b669-b472cb895ccf SUB_VIDEO VIDEOIDLE 300
powercfg /setacvalueindex 6fecc5ae-f350-48a5-b669-b472cb895ccf SUB_SLEEP STANDBYIDLE 300
powercfg /setactive 6fecc5ae-f350-48a5-b669-b472cb895ccf
```

Screen saver readback: `ScreenSaveActive=1`, `ScreenSaveTimeOut=300`, `ScreenSaverIsSecure=0`, `SCRNSAVE.EXE=C:\WINDOWS\system32\Ribbons.scr`, all unchanged. It has no on-resume logon requirement. The actual display timeout is independent and earlier than the saver; a black/animated saver was not substituted for display power-off.

Dynamic Lock `EnableGoodbye` was absent in HKCU Winlogon. `InactivityTimeoutSecs`, checked policy screen-saver lock values, MDM DeviceLock `MaxInactivityTimeDeviceLock`, and checked DynamicLock/HumanPresence policy values were not configured at the queried locations. User-presence prediction power setting was disabled. Read-only supported WinRT `HumanPresenceSettings.GetCurrentSettings()` reports **IsLockOnLeaveEnabled=false**, **IsWakeOnApproachEnabled=false**, **IsAdaptiveDimmingEnabled=false**. No enabled ordinary idle-lock control was found to switch off; authentication and sign-in-on-wake were preserved. These checks do not rule out every vendor or organization policy.

**Physical OLED panel-off and an unlocked interactive desktop were not observed.** `powercfg /requests` required administrator privileges, so a display power request that overrides idle-off could not be excluded. A LogonUI process existed in the current shell session; that alone is insufficient to determine the desktop's current lock state. No attempt was made to unlock the session or bypass security. Lid-open idle-unlocked behavior still needs an observed desktop check; closing the lid will sleep and require sign-in as before.

## Next safe acceptance steps

1. Establish an authorized runtime environment compatible with App Control; keep the current laptop's security policy intact. Run `./Windows/build.ps1 -Test` and record actual case results. Do not interpret static compilation as passing tests.
2. Run `EasyEject.Tests.exe --inventory` for native read-only mapping and compare with Windows Disk Management/CIM. Validate ABI layouts, notifications, descriptor failures, empty readers and hidden volumes. No removal requests in this check.
3. Inspect tray/UI at common DPI settings and observe AC idle panel power-off with lid open, session lock state, then protected lid-close sleep/sign-in. Shell checks can continue while the display is off or the desktop is locked.
4. Have Ryan explicitly identify a disposable card/reader before **any device-affecting test**. On that fixture, verify import hashes/original retention, conflicting paths, cancellation, multi-partition/LUN reservations, reconnect/format identity changes, reparse rejection, and open-file veto reporting. No real production card or SSD was qualified during this milestone.
5. Only after those checks should supported reader/card compatibility be claimed or further Mac parity/distribution work begin. Current code remains an unsigned prototype with no installer, auto-start, public release or Store proof.
